# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title conference Model

class Author {
    papers : List<Paper>
    calculateAcceptanceRate() : double
    countSubmittedPapers() : int
    submitPaper(paper : Paper) : void
}
class CoChair {
    makeFinalDecision(paper : Paper, decision : Grade) : int
}
class Paper {
    decision : Grade
    title : String
    type : PaperType
    reviews : List<ReviewAssignment>
    isAllReviewsConsistent() : boolean
}
class ReviewAssignment {
    feedback : String
    grade : Grade
    paper : Paper
}
class Reviewer {
    assignments : List<ReviewAssignment>
    calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore() : double
    calculateUnsubmittedReviews() : int
}
class User {
    name : String
    roles : List<UserRole>
}
abstract class UserRole {
    id : String
}
enum Grade {
    UNDECIDED
    ACCEPT
    REJECT
}
enum PaperType {
    RESEARCH
    EXPERIENCE
}

Author --|> UserRole
Author --> "*" Paper : papers
CoChair --|> UserRole
Paper --> "*" ReviewAssignment : reviews  'opposite: paper
ReviewAssignment --> Paper : paper  'opposite: reviews
Reviewer --|> UserRole
Reviewer --> "*" ReviewAssignment : assignments
User *-- "*" UserRole : roles
@enduml
```

Calculate the acceptance rate of papers submitted by this author.
Algorithm:
1. Let total = count of papers in Author.papers.
2. If total == 0, return 0.00.
3. Count accepted = number of papers whose decision == Grade.ACCEPT.
4. Compute rate = accepted / total as a double.
5. Return rate.
Related Features:
- papers
- countSubmittedPapers()
- Paper.decision
Related Classes: Paper, Grade
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Author instance and its papers list.
- Each paper decision should be one of Grade.UNDECIDED, Grade.ACCEPT, or Grade.REJECT.
Output:
- A double in range [0.0, 1.0].
- The value is the fraction of submitted papers that are accepted.
- If the author has no papers, return 0.00.
Pre-condition:
- The Author instance exists.
- Author.papers is initialized (possibly empty).

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title conference Model

class Author {
    papers : List<Paper>
    calculateAcceptanceRate() : double
    countSubmittedPapers() : int
    submitPaper(paper : Paper) : void
}

    makeFinalDecision(paper : Paper, decision : Grade) : int
}

    decision : Grade
    title : String
    type : PaperType
    reviews : List<ReviewAssignment>
    isAllReviewsConsistent() : boolean
}

    feedback : String
    grade : Grade
    paper : Paper
}

    assignments : List<ReviewAssignment>
    calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore() : double
    calculateUnsubmittedReviews() : int
}

    name : String
    roles : List<UserRole>
}

    id : String
}

    UNDECIDED
    ACCEPT
    REJECT
}

    RESEARCH
    EXPERIENCE

ReviewAssignment --> Paper : paper  'opposite: reviews

Reviewer --> "*" ReviewAssignment : assignments
User *-- "*" UserRole : roles
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
CoChair::makeFinalDecision
Summary:
Make the final decision for a paper only when review conditions are satisfied.
The decision can be recorded only if:
- the paper has at least 3 reviews,
- none of the reviews is UNDECIDED,
- all reviews are consistent (all ACCEPT or all REJECT).
Otherwise the paper's decision remains unchanged.
Algorithm:
1. If paper is null or decision is UNDECIDED, return -1 and leave paper.decision unchanged.
2. Let reviewCount = size of paper.reviews.
3. If reviewCount < 3, return -1.
4. If paper.isAllReviewsConsistent() is false, return -1.
5. Set paper.decision = decision.
6. Return 1.
Related Features:
- makeFinalDecision(paper, decision)
- Paper.decision
- Paper.reviews
- Paper.isAllReviewsConsistent()
Related Classes: Paper, ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
- paper: the Paper instance whose final decision is being made.
- decision: the final Grade to assign; expected to be ACCEPT or REJECT.
Output:
- int:
  - 1 if the decision is successfully set.
  - -1 if the conditions are not met.
Pre-condition:
- CoChair instance exists.
- paper is not null.
- paper.reviews is initialized.
- decision is either ACCEPT or REJECT; UNDECIDED is not a valid final decision.
Post-condition:
- If the method returns 1:
  - paper.decision is set to the provided decision.
- If the method returns -1:
  - paper.decision remains unchanged.
- No review assignments are modified.

# Operation Specifications
Paper::isAllReviewsConsistent
Summary:
Check whether all reviews for this paper are consistent.
Reviews are consistent only if every review has a submitted grade and all submitted grades are identical:
either all ACCEPT or all REJECT.
If there are no reviews or any review is UNDECIDED, return false.
Algorithm:
1. If paper.reviews is empty, return false.
2. Let firstSubmittedGrade = null.
3. For each review in paper.reviews:
   a. If review.grade == UNDECIDED, return false.
   b. If firstSubmittedGrade is null, store review.grade as firstSubmittedGrade.
   c. Otherwise, if review.grade != firstSubmittedGrade, return false.
4. Return true.
Related Features:
- reviews
- ReviewAssignment.grade
Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Paper instance and its reviews list.
Output:
- Boolean:
  - true if all reviews exist, none are UNDECIDED, and all grades are the same.
  - false otherwise.
Pre-condition:
- The Paper instance exists.
- Paper.reviews is initialized (possibly empty).
- Each review in reviews has a valid grade value.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- The returned value reflects the consistency of the current review grades.

```text
2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Operation Specifications
Paper::isAllReviewsConsistent
Summary:
Check whether all reviews for this paper are consistent.
Reviews are consistent only if every review has a submitted grade and all submitted grades are identical:
either all ACCEPT or all REJECT.
If there are no reviews or any review is UNDECIDED, return false.
Algorithm:
1. If paper.reviews is empty, return false.
2. Let firstSubmittedGrade = null.
3. For each review in paper.reviews:
   a. If review.grade == UNDECIDED, return false.
   b. If firstSubmittedGrade is null, store review.grade as firstSubmittedGrade.
   c. Otherwise, if review.grade != firstSubmittedGrade, return false.
4. Return true.
Related Features:
- reviews
- ReviewAssignment.grade
Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Paper instance and its reviews list.
Output:
- Boolean:
  - true if all reviews exist, none are UNDECIDED, and all grades are the same.
  - false otherwise.
Pre-condition:
- The Paper instance exists.
- Paper.reviews is initialized (possibly empty).
- Each review in reviews has a valid grade value.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- The returned value reflects the consistency of the current review grades.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Operation Specifications
Author::countSubmittedPapers
Summary:
Count the total number of papers submitted by this author.
Algorithm:
1. Let total = size of Author.papers.
2. Return total.
Related Features:
- papers
Related Classes: Paper
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Author instance and its papers list.
Output:
- An integer greater than or equal to 0.
- The number of papers associated with the author.
Pre-condition:
- The Author instance exists.
- Author.papers is initialized (possibly empty).
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- The returned value equals the current number of papers in Author.papers.

Author::calculateAcceptanceRate
Summary:
Calculate the acceptance rate of papers submitted by this author.
Algorithm:
1. Let total = count of papers in Author.papers.
2. If total == 0, return 0.00.
3. Count accepted = number of papers whose decision == Grade.ACCEPT.
4. Compute rate = accepted / total as a double.
5. Return rate.
Related Features:
- papers
- countSubmittedPapers()
- Paper.decision
Related Classes: Paper, Grade
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Author instance and its papers list.
- Each paper decision should be one of Grade.UNDECIDED, Grade.ACCEPT, or Grade.REJECT.
Output:
- A double in range [0.0, 1.0].
- The value is the fraction of submitted papers that are accepted.
- If the author has no papers, return 0.00.
Pre-condition:
- The Author instance exists.
- Author.papers is initialized (possibly empty).
- Each paper in papers has a valid decision value.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- The returned value equals accepted_papers / total_papers, or 0.00 when total_papers == 0.

Author::submitPaper
Summary:
Submit a paper by adding it to the author's paper list.
Algorithm:
1. If paper is null, do nothing or reject the call according to system policy.
2. Append paper to Author.papers if it is not already present.
3. Set paper's authorship association implicitly through the list relationship.
4. Return normally.
Related Features:
- papers
Related Classes: Paper
Input:
- paper: the Paper instance to be submitted.
- paper should be a valid Paper object with title and type defined.
Output:
- Void.
Pre-condition:
- The Author instance exists.
- Author.papers is initialized.
- paper is not null.
- The paper is a valid paper object.
Post-condition:
- The paper is included in Author.papers after the call.
- The size of papers increases by 1 if the paper was not already present.
- No other state is changed.

The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

```text
3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.
```



# Operation Specifications
Reviewer::calculateUnsubmittedReviews
Summary:
Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews assigned to this reviewer.
A review is unsubmitted when its grade is UNDECIDED.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize count = 0.
2. For each assignment in Reviewer.assignments:
   a. If assignment.grade == UNDECIDED, increment count.
3. Return count.
Related Features:
- assignments
- ReviewAssignment.grade
Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Reviewer instance and its assignments list.
Output:
- An integer greater than or equal to 0.
- The number of assigned reviews whose grade is UNDECIDED.
- If there are no assignments, return 0.
Pre-condition:
- The Reviewer instance exists.
- Reviewer.assignments is initialized (possibly empty).
- Each assignment has a valid grade value.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- The returned value equals the number of UNDECIDED assignments.

Reviewer::calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore
Summary:
Calculate the average score of submitted review grades for this reviewer.
Only submitted reviews are considered:
ACCEPT is treated as 1, REJECT is treated as 0, and UNDECIDED reviews are excluded.
If there are no submitted reviews, return 0.00.
Algorithm:
1. Let submittedCount = 0 and scoreSum = 0.
2. For each assignment in assignments:
   a. If assignment.grade == UNDECIDED, continue.
   b. If assignment.grade == ACCEPT, add 1 to scoreSum.
   c. If assignment.grade == REJECT, add 0 to scoreSum.
   d. Increment submittedCount.
3. If submittedCount == 0, return 0.00.
4. Return scoreSum / submittedCount as a double.
Related Features:
- assignments
- ReviewAssignment.grade
Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
- No explicit input parameters.
- Uses the current Reviewer instance and its assignments list.
Output:
- double:
  - A value in the range [0.0, 1.0].
  - Computed as the average of submitted grades, where ACCEPT = 1 and REJECT = 0.
  - Returns 0.00 when there are no submitted reviews.
Pre-condition:
- The Reviewer instance exists.
- Reviewer.assignments is initialized (possibly empty).
- Each assignment has a valid grade value.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- The returned value reflects the average score of submitted reviews, with an error margin of 0.001.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

```java
  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
```
```java
   * Calculate the average score of submitted review grades for this reviewer.
   * Only submitted reviews are considered:
   * ACCEPT is treated as 1, REJECT is treated as 0, and UNDECIDED reviews are excluded.
   * If there are no submitted reviews, return 0.00.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Let submittedCount = 0 and scoreSum = 0.
   * 2. For each assignment in assignments:
   *    a. If assignment.grade == UNDECIDED, continue.
   *    b. If assignment.grade == ACCEPT, add 1 to scoreSum.
   *    c. If assignment.grade == REJECT, add 0 to scoreSum.
   *    d. Increment submittedCount.
   * 3. If submittedCount == 0, return 0.00.
   * 4. Return scoreSum / submittedCount as a double.
   * Related Features:
   * - assignments
   * - ReviewAssignment.grade
   * Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
   * Input:
```

   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore()
  {
    int submittedCount = 0;
    int scoreSum = 0;
    for (ReviewAssignment assignment : getAssignments())
    {
      Grade grade = assignment.getGrade();
      if (grade == Grade.UNDECIDED)
      {
        continue;
      }
      if (grade == Grade.ACCEPT)
      {
        scoreSum += 1;
      }
      submittedCount++;
    }
    if (submittedCount == 0)
    {
      return 0.0;
    }
    return (double)scoreSum / submittedCount;
    
  }

   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Make the final decision for a paper.
   * Return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all conditions are met:
   * 1) the paper has at least three reviews,
   * 2) no review is UNDECIDED,
   * 3) all reviews are consistent.
   * Otherwise, return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If paper.reviews.size < 3, return -1.
   * 2. If paper.isAllReviewsConsistent() is false, return -1.
   * 3. Set paper.decision to decision.
   * 4. Return 1.
   * Related Features:
   * - makeFinalDecision(paper : Paper, decision : Grade) : int
   * - Paper.decision
   * - Paper.reviews
   * - Paper.isAllReviewsConsistent()
   * Related Classes: Paper, Grade
   * Input:
   * - paper: the Paper whose final decision is being made.
   * - decision: the final Grade to assign to the paper; expected to be ACCEPT or REJECT.
   * Output:
   * - int:
   *   - 1 if the decision is successfully made and stored in paper.decision.
   *   - -1 if the paper does not satisfy the required conditions; paper.decision remains unchanged.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - The CoChair instance exists.
   * - paper is not null.
   * - paper.reviews is initialized.
   * - decision is a valid Grade value.
   * Post-condition:
   * - If the paper has at least three consistent, non-UNDECIDED reviews, paper.decision is set to the given decision and 1 is returned.
   * - Otherwise, paper.decision is unchanged and -1 is returned.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public int makeFinalDecision(final Paper paper, final Grade decision)
  {
    if (paper.getReviews().size() < 3)
    {
      return -1;
    }
    if (!paper.isAllReviewsConsistent())
    {
      return -1;
    }
    paper.setDecision(decision);
    return 1;
  }

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
   * Summary:
   * Check whether all reviews for this paper are consistent.
   * Reviews are consistent only if:
   * 1) the paper has at least three reviews,
   * 2) no review grade is UNDECIDED,
   * 3) all submitted grades are identical, i.e., all ACCEPT or all REJECT.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If paper.reviews is empty or paper.reviews.size < 3, return false.
   * 2. Let firstSubmittedGrade = null.
   * 3. For each review in paper.reviews:
   *    a. If review.grade == UNDECIDED, return false.
   *    b. If firstSubmittedGrade is null, store review.grade as firstSubmittedGrade.
   *    c. Otherwise, if review.grade != firstSubmittedGrade, return false.
   * 4. Return true.
   * Related Features:
   * - reviews
   * - ReviewAssignment.grade
   * Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
```

   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean isAllReviewsConsistent()
  {
    if (reviews == null || reviews.size() < 3)
    {
      return false;
    }

    Grade firstSubmittedGrade = null;
    for (ReviewAssignment review : reviews)
    {
      Grade grade = review.getGrade();
      if (grade == Grade.UNDECIDED)
      {
        return false;
      }

      if (firstSubmittedGrade == null)
      {
        firstSubmittedGrade = grade;
      }
      else if (grade != firstSubmittedGrade)
      {
        return false;
      }
    }

    return true;
  }

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title conference Model

class Author {
    papers : List<Paper>
    calculateAcceptanceRate() : double
    countSubmittedPapers() : int
    submitPaper(paper : Paper) : void
}
class CoChair {
    makeFinalDecision(paper : Paper, decision : Grade) : int
}
class Paper {
    decision : Grade
    title : String
    type : PaperType
    reviews : List<ReviewAssignment>
    isAllReviewsConsistent() : boolean
}
class ReviewAssignment {
    feedback : String
    grade : Grade
    paper : Paper
}
class Reviewer {
    assignments : List<ReviewAssignment>
    calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore() : double
    calculateUnsubmittedReviews() : int
}
class User {
    name : String
    roles : List<UserRole>
}
abstract class UserRole {
    id : String
}
enum Grade {
    UNDECIDED
    ACCEPT
    REJECT
}
enum PaperType {
    RESEARCH
    EXPERIENCE
}

Author --|> UserRole
Author --> "*" Paper : papers
CoChair --|> UserRole
Paper --> "*" ReviewAssignment : reviews  'opposite: paper
ReviewAssignment --> Paper : paper  'opposite: reviews
Reviewer --|> UserRole
Reviewer --> "*" ReviewAssignment : assignments
User *-- "*" UserRole : roles
@enduml
```

96285 [main] INFO  du.ustb.sei.mde.mwe2.EcoreGenerator  - LLM Response:
{org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@6f6a2ac8 (name: calculateAcceptanceRate) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary:
Calculates the acceptance rate of papers submitted by this author as:
  number of accepted papers / total number of submitted papers.
A paper is counted as accepted when its decision is ACCEPT.
Algorithm:
1. Let total := size of papers.
2. If total = 0, return 0.0.
3. Count accepted := number of papers whose decision == ACCEPT.
4. Compute rate := accepted / total as double.
5. Return rate.
Related Features:
- papers
- Paper.decision
Related Classes: Paper, Grade
Input:
- None.
- Uses the author's current papers collection.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title conference Model

class Author {
    papers : List<Paper>
    calculateAcceptanceRate() : double
    countSubmittedPapers() : int
    submitPaper(paper : Paper) : void
}
class CoChair {
    makeFinalDecision(paper : Paper, decision : Grade) : int
}
class Paper {
    decision : Grade
    title : String
    type : PaperType
    reviews : List<ReviewAssignment>
    isAllReviewsConsistent() : boolean
}
class ReviewAssignment {
    feedback : String
    grade : Grade
    paper : Paper
}
class Reviewer {
    assignments : List<ReviewAssignment>
    calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore() : double
    calculateUnsubmittedReviews() : int
}
class User {
    name : String
    roles : List<UserRole>
}
abstract class UserRole {
    id : String
}
enum Grade {
    UNDECIDED
    ACCEPT
    REJECT
}
enum PaperType {
    RESEARCH
    EXPERIENCE
}

Author --|> UserRole
Author --> "*" Paper : papers
CoChair --|> UserRole
Paper --> "*" ReviewAssignment : reviews  'opposite: paper
ReviewAssignment --> Paper : paper  'opposite: reviews
Reviewer --|> UserRole
Reviewer --> "*" ReviewAssignment : assignments
User *-- "*" UserRole : roles
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Reviewer::calculateUnsubmittedReviews
Summary:
Counts the number of unsubmitted reviews assigned to this reviewer.
A review is unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED.
Algorithm:
1. Let count := 0.
2. For each assignment in assignments:
   a. If assignment.grade == UNDECIDED, increment count.
3. Return count.
Related Features:
- assignments
- ReviewAssignment.grade
Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
- None.
- Uses the reviewer's current assignments collection.
Output:
- An integer count of unsubmitted reviews.
- Range: 0 or greater.
- Returns 0 if there are no assignments.
Pre-condition:
- assignments is initialized.
- Each assignment has a valid grade.
Post-condition:
- No system state is changed.
- The returned value equals the number of assignments whose grade is UNDECIDED.

Reviewer::calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore
Summary:
Computes the average numerical score of this reviewer's submitted reviews only.
Grade mapping:
  ACCEPT -> 1.0
  REJECT -> 0.0
  UNDECIDED -> ignored
The result is a value between 0.0 and 1.0.
If the reviewer has no submitted reviews, return 0.0.
Algorithm:
1. Let sum := 0.0 and count := 0.
2. For each assignment in assignments:
   a. If assignment.grade == ACCEPT, add 1.0 to sum and increment count.
   b. If assignment.grade == REJECT, add 0.0 to sum and increment count.
   c. If assignment.grade == UNDECIDED, ignore it.
3. If count == 0, return 0.0.
4. Return sum / count.
Related Features:
- assignments
- ReviewAssignment.grade
Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
- None.
- Uses the reviewer's current assignments collection.
Output:
- A double in range [0.0, 1.0].
- Returns 0.0 when there are no submitted reviews.
- Precision should be within 0.001.
Pre-condition:
- assignments is initialized.
- Each assignment has a valid grade.
Post-condition:
- No system state is changed.
- The returned value equals the average of submitted review grades only.

```text
4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Operation Specifications
Author::submitPaper
Summary:
Submits a paper by adding it to this author's paper list.
This operation models that an author can submit as many papers as desired.
Algorithm:
1. Verify paper is not null.
2. Append paper to papers.
3. Optionally ensure the paper is associated with this author in the system model if such linkage exists externally.
Related Features:
- papers
Related Classes: Paper
Input:
- paper: a Paper instance to be submitted.
Output:
- None.
Pre-condition:
- paper is not null.
- papers is initialized.
- The paper is not required to have any reviews or decision before submission.
Post-condition:
- paper is added to the author's papers collection.
- The size of papers increases by 1.
- No paper decision is changed by this operation.

Author::calculateAcceptanceRate
Summary:
Calculates the acceptance rate of papers submitted by this author as:
  number of accepted papers / total number of submitted papers.
A paper is counted as accepted when its decision is ACCEPT.
Algorithm:
1. Let total := size of papers.
2. If total = 0, return 0.0.
3. Count accepted := number of papers whose decision == ACCEPT.
4. Compute rate := accepted / total as double.
5. Return rate.
Related Features:
- papers
- Paper.decision
Related Classes: Paper, Grade
Input:
- None.
- Uses the author's current papers collection.
Output:
- A double in range [0.0, 1.0].
- Returns 0.0 when the author has no papers.
Pre-condition:
- papers is initialized and contains zero or more Paper objects.
- Each Paper has a valid decision value in Grade.
Post-condition:
- No system state is changed.
- The returned value equals accepted papers / total papers, or 0.0 if total is 0.

Author::countSubmittedPapers
Summary:
Counts how many papers this author has submitted.
Algorithm:
1. Let count := size of papers.
2. Return count.
Related Features:
- papers
Related Classes: Paper
Input:
- None.
- Uses the author's current papers collection.
Output:
- An integer count of submitted papers.
- Range: 0 or greater.
Pre-condition:
- papers is initialized.
Post-condition:
- No system state is changed.
- The returned value equals the number of papers in papers.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

Paper::isAllReviewsConsistent
Summary:
Checks whether all reviews for this paper are consistent.
Reviews are consistent only when:
- the paper has at least three reviews;
- none of the reviews has grade UNDECIDED;
- all review grades are exclusively ACCEPT or exclusively REJECT.
If the paper has no reviews, or any review is UNDECIDED, the result is false.
Algorithm:
1. If reviews is empty, return false.
2. If size of reviews < 3, return false.
3. If any review in reviews has grade == UNDECIDED, return false.
4. Let first := grade of the first review.
5. Return true if every review in reviews has the same grade as first; otherwise return false.

- reviews
- ReviewAssignment.grade

- No system state is changed.

# Operation Specifications
Paper::isAllReviewsConsistent
Summary:
Checks whether all reviews for this paper are consistent.
Reviews are consistent only when:
- the paper has at least three reviews;
- none of the reviews has grade UNDECIDED;
- all review grades are exclusively ACCEPT or exclusively REJECT.
If the paper has no reviews, or any review is UNDECIDED, the result is false.
Algorithm:
1. If reviews is empty, return false.
2. If size of reviews < 3, return false.
3. If any review in reviews has grade == UNDECIDED, return false.
4. Let first := grade of the first review.
5. Return true if every review in reviews has the same grade as first; otherwise return false.
Related Features:
- reviews
- ReviewAssignment.grade
Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
- None.
- Uses this paper's current reviews collection.
Output:
- A boolean value.
- Returns true only when all reviews are consistent under the stated rules.
Pre-condition:
- reviews is initialized and contains zero or more ReviewAssignment objects.
- Each ReviewAssignment has a valid grade value in Grade.
Post-condition:
- No system state is changed.
- The returned value reflects whether the paper's reviews are consistent.

# Requirement
```text
To help the program chair, we are planning to develop a computer-assisted review system. The system will be available to users who can be authors, reviewers, and co-chairs. Each user has a name. Each user role (author, reviewer, or co-chair) has an id to identify the role. Papers are known by the title and whether they are research papers or experience report papers. The paper is reviewed by at least three reviewers. On the other side, the authors can submit as many papers as they want. When a reviewer logs in, she can consult the list of papers that have been assigned to her for review, and submit reviews for each of those papers. Each review has feedback as well as grade. Before submission the grade is UNDECIDED, and after submission it becomes ACCEPT or REJECT. A final decision is made by the co-chair for each paper.
Functional Requirement:
1.Calculate the number of unsubmitted reviews for a given reviewer. A review is considered unsubmitted if its grade is UNDECIDED. Return 0 if all reviews are submitted by the reviewer or if the reviewer has no assignments.

2.Before the chair makes the final decision, check whether all reviews for a paper (from at least three reviewers) are either exclusively Accept or exclusively Reject. Return a boolean value. If a paper has no reviews, or if any review is UNDECIDED, the reviews are not considered consistent (return false). The CoChair's makeFinalDecision method should return 1 and set the paper's decision only when all three conditions are met: at least 3 reviews, no UNDECIDED reviews, and all reviews are consistent; otherwise return -1 and leave the paper's decision unchanged.

3.Count the total number of papers submitted by an author.

4.Calculate the acceptance rate of papers for an author. Example: If an author submitted 10 papers with 6 accepted, return 0.60. If the author has no papers, return 0.00.

5.Convert a reviewer's submitted review grades into a numerical average score between 0.0 and 1.0, considering only submitted reviews (excluding UNDECIDED) and treating ACCEPT as 1 and REJECT as 0. For example, for 3 ACCEPT and 1 REJECT, the average score is (3×1 + 1×0)/4 = 0.75. The result should be within an error margin of 0.001. If there are no submitted reviews (all UNDECIDED or no assignments), return 0.00.


```

# Operation Specifications
CoChair::makeFinalDecision
Summary:
Makes the final decision for a paper only when all required review conditions are satisfied.
The decision is applied only if:
- the paper has at least three reviews;
- none of the reviews is UNDECIDED;
- all reviews are consistent (all ACCEPT or all REJECT).
Returns 1 when the decision is set successfully; otherwise returns -1 and leaves the paper unchanged.
Algorithm:
1. If paper has fewer than 3 reviews, return -1.
2. If paper.isAllReviewsConsistent() is false, return -1.
3. If any review in paper.reviews has grade == UNDECIDED, return -1.
4. Set paper.decision to decision.
5. Return 1.
Related Features:
- Paper.decision
- Paper.reviews
- Paper.isAllReviewsConsistent()
Related Classes: Paper, ReviewAssignment, Grade
Input:
- paper: the Paper whose final decision is to be made; must not be null.
- decision: the final decision grade to set for the paper; should be ACCEPT or REJECT.
Output:
- 1 if the paper's decision is updated successfully.
- -1 if the paper does not satisfy the required conditions; in this case paper.decision is unchanged.
Pre-condition:
- paper is initialized and its reviews collection is available.
- decision is a valid final decision value, typically ACCEPT or REJECT.
Post-condition:
- If the operation returns 1, paper.decision is set to decision.
- If the operation returns -1, paper.decision remains unchanged.

```java
   * If the author has no papers, returns 0.00.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If papers is empty, return 0.00.
   * 2. Count accepted papers in papers where paper.decision == ACCEPT.
```

  {
    EList<Paper> papers = getPapers();

    {
      if (paper != null && paper.getDecision() == Grade.ACCEPT)

        acceptedCount++;
      }
    }
    return (double) acceptedCount / papers.size();
  }

  {

    for (ReviewAssignment assignment : getAssignments())
    {
      if (assignment.getGrade() == Grade.UNDECIDED)
      {
        count++;
      }
    }
    return count;
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->

   */
  public double calculateSubmittedReviewAverageScore()
  {
    int sum = 0;
    int count = 0;
    for (ReviewAssignment assignment : getAssignments())
    {
      if (assignment.getGrade() == Grade.ACCEPT)
      {
        sum += 1;
        count++;
      }
      else if (assignment.getGrade() == Grade.REJECT)
      {
        count++;
      }
    }
    if (count == 0)
    {
      return 0.0;
    }
    return (double)sum / count;
    
  }

public class CoChair extends UserRole
{
  protected CoChair()
  { ... /* omitted */ }

  @Override
  protected EClass eStaticClass()
  { ... /* omitted */ }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Makes the final decision for a paper only when all required review conditions are satisfied.
   * The decision is applied only if:
   * - the paper has at least three reviews;
   * - none of the reviews is UNDECIDED;
   * - all reviews are consistent (all ACCEPT or all REJECT).
   * Returns 1 when the decision is set successfully; otherwise returns -1 and leaves the paper unchanged.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If paper has fewer than 3 reviews, return -1.
   * 2. If paper.isAllReviewsConsistent() is false, return -1.
   * 3. If any review in paper.reviews has grade == UNDECIDED, return -1.
   * 4. Set paper.decision to decision.
   * 5. Return 1.
   * Related Features:
   * - Paper.decision
   * - Paper.reviews
   * - Paper.isAllReviewsConsistent()
   * Related Classes: Paper, ReviewAssignment, Grade
   * Input:
   * - paper: the Paper whose final decision is to be made; must not be null.
   * - decision: the final decision grade to set for the paper; should be ACCEPT or REJECT.
   * Output:
   * - 1 if the paper's decision is updated successfully.
   * - -1 if the paper does not satisfy the required conditions; in this case paper.decision is unchanged.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - paper is initialized and its reviews collection is available.
   * - decision is a valid final decision value, typically ACCEPT or REJECT.
   * Post-condition:
   * - If the operation returns 1, paper.decision is set to decision.
   * - If the operation returns -1, paper.decision remains unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public int makeFinalDecision(final Paper paper, final Grade decision)
  {
    if (paper.getReviews().size() < 3)
    {
      return -1;
    }
    if (!paper.isAllReviewsConsistent())
    {
      return -1;
    }
    for (ReviewAssignment review : paper.getReviews())
    {
      if (review.getGrade() == Grade.UNDECIDED)
      {
        return -1;
      }
    }
    paper.setDecision(decision);
    return 1;
  }

} // CoChair

   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary:
   * Checks whether all reviews for this paper are consistent.
   * Reviews are consistent only when:
   * - the paper has at least three reviews;
   * - none of the reviews has grade UNDECIDED;
   * - all review grades are exclusively ACCEPT or exclusively REJECT.
   * If the paper has no reviews, or any review is UNDECIDED, the result is false.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If reviews is empty, return false.
   * 2. If size of reviews < 3, return false.
   * 3. If any review in reviews has grade == UNDECIDED, return false.
   * 4. Let first := grade of the first review.
   * 5. Return true if every review in reviews has the same grade as first; otherwise return false.
   * Related Features:
   * - reviews
   * - ReviewAssignment.grade
   * Related Classes: ReviewAssignment, Grade
   * Input:
   * - None.
   * - Uses this paper's current reviews collection.
   * Output:
   * - A boolean value.
   * - Returns true only when all reviews are consistent under the stated rules.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - reviews is initialized and contains zero or more ReviewAssignment objects.
   * - Each ReviewAssignment has a valid grade value in Grade.
   * Post-condition:
   * - No system state is changed.
   * - The returned value reflects whether the paper's reviews are consistent.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model kind="operation"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean isAllReviewsConsistent()
  {
    if (reviews == null || reviews.isEmpty())
    {
      return false;
    }
    if (reviews.size() < 3)
    {
      return false;
    }

    Grade first = null;
    for (ReviewAssignment review : reviews)
    {
      Grade grade = review.getGrade();
      if (grade == Grade.UNDECIDED)
      {
        return false;
      }
      if (first == null)
      {
        first = grade;
      }
      else if (grade != first)
      {
        return false;
      }
    }
    return true;
  }
